# GTA MLI Select early-file professionals — 80 personalized partnership dossiers

Verified 2026-07-15. Exactly 80 net-new GTA prospects: 20 architects, 20 planners/designers, 20 technical/cost/performance professionals, and 20 multifamily real-estate/feasibility/valuation advisors. Every record passed the consent-and-confidentiality gate and scored at least 6/10. Public evidence supports qualification; it does not prove partnership interest. Re-verify live claims, titles and routes immediately before outreach.

## 1. Appraisal Source Canada

**Lane:** Multifamily Real Estate / Feasibility / Valuation Advisor · **territory:** Toronto and the GTA · **fit:** 99/100 · **eligibility:** 10/10  
**Target:** AACI commercial appraisal lead  
**Public routes:** info@appraisalsource.ca; rf@appraisalsource.ca; lm@appraisalsource.ca; mc@appraisalsource.ca; lp@appraisalsource.ca · 416.297.1699 · [contact/site](https://appraisalsource.ca/)  

**Official evidence.** Licensed Toronto & GTA Commercial (AACI) and Residential (CRA) Real Estate Appraisal Firm. Approved by major banks and lenders for 30+ years. Discuss Your Real Estate Appraisal Needs & Timing and receive a free quotation. Fully AACI and CRA certified. Contact ASC Today. We provide fast, affordable and reliable real estate appraisals (both comprehensive Commercial and Residential Appraisals) for all purposes.

**MLI Select touchpoint.** commercial, land and multi-residential appraisal for banks and private lenders during financing underwriting.

**Early-file artifacts.** as-is/as-complete value assumptions, unit mix, rent roll, market evidence, costs and lender/CMHC reliance requirements.

**Personalized hook.** Appraisal Source Canada's official site describes this relevant mandate: “Licensed Toronto & GTA Commercial (AACI) and Residential (CRA) Real Estate Appraisal Firm. Approved by major banks and lenders for 30+ years.” That puts the team close to commercial, land and multi-residential appraisal for banks and private lenders while the owner can still change the financing plan.

**Offer.** A consent-based appraisal-to-underwriting handoff: FairLend handles the owner’s MLI Select and construction-financing analysis, while DrawFlow maps the accepted budget and development milestones to proposed advances without affecting the appraiser’s independence or reliance terms.

**CTA.** Would AACI commercial appraisal lead spend 20 minutes reviewing one anonymized GTA rental scenario and decide whether a consent-based referral pilot is useful?

**Email script.**

Subject: an MLI Select checkpoint for Appraisal Source Canada's early rental files

Hi there — Appraisal Source Canada's official site describes this relevant mandate: “Licensed Toronto & GTA Commercial (AACI) and Residential (CRA) Real Estate Appraisal Firm. Because your team is engaged during financing underwriting, you can see commercial, land and multi-residential appraisal for banks and private lenders before the owner’s financing plan is fixed. FairLend can add an optional, consent-based MLI Select and construction-financing checkpoint without asking you to disclose a client file or step into lending advice. **LinkedIn / DM.** Appraisal Source Canada's official site describes this relevant mandate: “Licensed Toronto & GTA Commercial (AACI) and Residential (CRA) Real Estate Appraisal Firm. Approved by major banks and lenders for 30+ years.” That puts the team close to commercial, land and multi-residential appraisal for banks and private lenders while the owner can still change the financing plan. FairLend can give consenting rental clients an early MLI Select financing screen, while DrawFlow maps the evolving budget and milestones to proposed advances. Open to a 20-minute anonymized workflow review?

**Phone / form.** Ask for AACI commercial appraisal lead. Lead with the official-site signal: “Appraisal Source Canada's official site describes this relevant mandate: “Licensed Toronto & GTA Commercial (AACI) and Residential (CRA) Real Estate Appraisal Firm. Approved by major banks and lenders for 30+ years.” That puts the team close to commercial, land and multi-residential appraisal for ba” Then explain that FairLend offers an optional, owner-authorized MLI Select financing checkpoint and DrawFlow budget/draw roadmap. Request a 20-minute anonymized workflow review; do not request project documents on the first touch.

**Follow-up sequence.**

Day 3 — send a one-page role-specific MLI Select evidence map showing the exact handoff point and the no-file-without-consent rule.
Day 8 — share a worked anonymized example using as-is/as-complete value assumptions, unit mix, rent roll, market evidence, costs and lender/CMHC reliance requirements.
Day 15 — ask whether one consenting owner/client should test the workflow; offer a secure intake link or a three-way introduction.
Day 25 — close the loop politely and move to quarterly nurture if there is no reply.

**Asset.** Multifamily Real Estate / Feasibility / Valuation Advisor MLI Select early-file checklist + anonymized DrawFlow financing underwriting-to-advance example.

**Risks.** Likely concern: another financing vendor could disrupt the client relationship or create scope/liability ambiguity. Frame FairLend as optional and consent-based, preserve Appraisal Source Canada's professional role, use an anonymized first review, and make the client—not the consultant—the source of confidential financing information.

**Guardrails.** Never ask for or accept a client file without owner/borrower authorization. Do not imply CMHC approval, guaranteed financing, rates, eligibility, or that the prospect endorses FairLend. Preserve the prospect’s professional independence and never ask an agent, appraiser, architect, planner or consultant to breach confidentiality, fiduciary duty, privilege, reliance terms or procurement rules.

**Sources.** [appraisalsource.ca](https://appraisalsource.ca/) · [appraisalsource.ca](https://appraisalsource.ca/services-2/) · [appraisalsource.ca](https://appraisalsource.ca/services/) · [appraisalsource.ca](https://appraisalsource.ca/about-us/)

**Phone / form.** Ask for Principal accessibility consultant. Lead with the official-site signal: “DesignABLE’s credited work on Toronto’s shelter design guidelines demonstrates that the team influences accessibility requirements at the standards-and-drawing stage, where MLI Select accessibility commitments must be designed deliberately.” Then explain that FairLend offers an optional, owner-authorized MLI Select financing checkpoint and DrawFlow budget/draw roadmap. Request a 20-minute anonymized workflow review; do not request project documents on the first touch.

**Follow-up sequence.**

Day 3 — send a one-page role-specific MLI Select evidence map showing the exact handoff point and the no-file-without-consent rule.
Day 8 — share a worked anonymized example using accessible-unit/visitability strategy, feature schedule, drawing-review status and architect/consultant confirmation.
Day 15 — ask whether one consenting owner/client should test the workflow; offer a secure intake link or a three-way introduction.
Day 25 — close the loop politely and move to quarterly nurture if there is no reply.

**Asset.** Technical / Cost / Performance Professional MLI Select early-file checklist + anonymized DrawFlow concept and schematic design-to-advance example.

**Risks.** Likely concern: another financing vendor could disrupt the client relationship or create scope/liability ambiguity. Frame FairLend as optional and consent-based, preserve DesignABLE Environments's professional role, use an anonymized first review, and make the client—not the consultant—the source of confidential financing information.

**Guardrails.** Never ask for or accept a client file without owner/borrower authorization. Do not imply CMHC approval, guaranteed financing, rates, eligibility, or that the prospect endorses FairLend. Preserve the prospect’s professional independence and never ask an agent, appraiser, architect, planner or consultant to breach confidentiality, fiduciary duty, privilege, reliance terms or procurement rules.
